27.2
Hyperparameter Separability

The results in the previous section establish the importance of optimizing
\epsilon
when using adaptive methods, and how not tuning
\epsilon
can be a confounding factor when comparing different adaptive optimizers.

A key obstacle to proper tuning of
\epsilon
is its interaction with the learning rate
\alpha
: as discussed in Section
25.2
, ‘emulating’ SGD with a learning rate
\gamma
can be done by setting
\alpha=\gamma\epsilon
in Adam and then increasing
\epsilon
: once its value is large enough (compared to
v_{t}
), scaling up
\epsilon
any further will not affect Adam’s behavior as long as
\alpha
is scaled up by the same multiplicative factor. Conversely, when
\epsilon
is small (compared to components of
v_{t}
), we have that
\sqrt{v_{t}}+\epsilon\approx\sqrt{v_{t}}
, hence decreasing
\epsilon
even further will not affect the optimization dynamics as long as
\alpha
remains fixed.

This suggests the existence of two distinct regimes for Adam (and other adaptive methods): an adaptive regime, when
\epsilon
is small and there is no interaction between
\alpha
and
\epsilon
, and a non-adaptive regime, when
\epsilon
is large and the learning rate
\alpha
must scale linearly with
\epsilon
to preserve the optimization dynamics. The exact phase transition is governed by
v_{t}

i.e.,
the second moments of the gradients, which depends not only on the task but also on the model.

By normalizing the parameter-wise learning rates
\eta_{t}
at each iteration, AvaGrad guarantees that the magnitude of the effective learning rates is independent of
\epsilon
, essentially decoupling it from
\alpha
. With AvaGrad,
\alpha
governs optimization dynamics in both regimes: when
\epsilon
is small, changing its value has negligible impact on
\eta_{t}
and
\|\eta_{t}\|
, hence the updates will be the same, while in the non-adaptive regime we have that
\eta_{t}\approx[\frac{1}{\epsilon},\frac{1}{\epsilon},\dots]
and
\|\eta_{t}/\sqrt{d}\|_{2}\approx\frac{1}{\epsilon}
, hence normalizing
\eta_{t}
yields an all-ones vector regardless of
\epsilon
(as long as it remains large enough compared to all components of
v_{t}
).

Figures
34
and
35
show the performance of a Wide ResNet 28-4 on CIFAR-10 when trained with Adam and AvaGrad, for different
(\alpha,\epsilon)
configurations
i.e.,
the grid search employed for CIFAR, described in Section
26.3
. For Adam, the non-adaptive regime is indeed characterized by a linear relation between
\alpha
and
\epsilon
, while its performance in the adaptive regime depends mostly on
\alpha
alone. AvaGrad offers decoupling between the two parameters, with
\alpha
precisely characterizing the non-adaptive regime (
i.e.,
the performance is independent of
\epsilon
) while almost fully describing the adaptive regime as well, except for regions close to the phase transition. For each of the
21
different values of
\epsilon
, AvaGrad performed best with
\alpha=1.0
.

Figure 38
:

Iterations to achieve
0.5\%
suboptimality, measured in terms of validation accuracy on CIFAR-10, for Adam and AvaGrad when tuning
\alpha
and
\epsilon
with various standard hyperparameter optimizers.

Figure 39
:

Suboptimality (gap in validation accuracy) when optimizing
\alpha
and
\epsilon
with GLD/CGLD, as a function of trials (
i.e.,
validation accuracy evaluations for a value of
(\alpha,\epsilon)
): AvaGrad is significantly cheaper to tune than Adam, being especially efficient when adopting Coordinate GLD due to its hyperparameter separability.

27.3
Hyperparameter Optimization

To assess our hypothesis that AvaGrad offers hyperparameter decoupling, which enables
\alpha
and
\epsilon
to be tuned
independently
via two line-search procedures instead of a grid search, we compare tuning costs of Adam and AvaGrad with prominent hyperparameter optimization methods such as Parzen Trees and CMA-ES. We also consider Gradientless Descent (GLD)
(
Golovin et al. 2020
)
, a powerful zeroth-order method.

We frame the task of tuning
\alpha
and
\epsilon
as a 2D optimization problem with a
21\times 21
discrete domain representing all
(\alpha,\epsilon)
configurations for CIFAR discussed in Section
26.3
, with the minimization objective being the error of a Wide ResNet-28-4 on CIFAR-10 when trained with the corresponding
(\alpha,\epsilon)
values.

Figure
38
shows the number of iterations required by different hyperparameter optimizers to achieve
0.5\%
suboptimality
i.e.,
an error at most
0.5\%
higher than the lowest achieved in the grid. AvaGrad is significantly cheaper to tune than Adam, regardless of the adopted tuning algorithm, including random search – showing that AvaGrad is able to perform well with a wider range of hyperparameter values.

We also consider a variant of GLD where descent steps on
\alpha
and
\epsilon
are performed separately in an alternating manner, akin to coordinate descent
(
Larochelle et al. 2007
;
Nesterov 2012
)
. This variant, which we denote by CGLD, is in principle well-suited for problems where variables have independent contributions to the objective, as is approximately the case for AvaGrad. Results are given in Figure
39
: AvaGrad achieves less than
1\%
suboptimality in 13 iterations when tuned with CGLD, while Adam requires
74
with GLD. As expected, coordinate-wise updates result in considerably faster tuning for AvaGrad.

28
Analysis

Figure 40
:

The mean gradient norm as function of the iteration
t
when optimizing Equation (
70
). Matching our theoretical results, Delayed Adam and Adam with dynamic
\epsilon_{t}
both converge, while Adam fails to converge.

28.1
Convergence

Lastly, we demonstrate our convergence results in Theorems
6.2
and
6.3
experimentally by employing Adam, AMSGrad, Adam with a 1-step delay (Delayed Adam), and Adam with
\epsilon_{t}=\sqrt{t^{3}}
, on a synthetic problem with the same form as the one used in the proof of Theorem
6.1
:

\begin{split}\min_{w\in[0,1]}f(w),\quad\quad f_{s}(w)=\begin{cases}999\frac{w^{2}}{2},\text{ w.p. }\frac{1}{500}\\
-w,\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}\end{split}

(70)

where
f(w)\coloneqq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f_{s}(w)}\right]
.

This problem admits a stationary point
w^{\star}\approx 0.5
, and satisfies
Theorem
6.2
for
\beta_{1}=0,\beta_{2}=0.99,\epsilon=10^{-8}
. Figure
40
presents
\frac{1}{t}\sum_{t^{\prime}=1}^{t}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t^{\prime}})}\right\rVert^{2}
during training, and shows that Adam fails to converge (Theorem
6.1
), while both Delayed Adam and dynamic Adam converge successfully (Theorems
6.3
and
6.2
). We attribute the faster convergence of Delayed Adam to the lack of constraints on the parameter-wise learning rates.

29
Discussion

29.1
Contributions

This chapter makes several key contributions:

•

We show that Adam can
provably converge
for non-convex problems given a proper tuning of its adaptability parameter
\epsilon
. We address the apparent contradiction with
Reddi et al. 2018
, providing new insights on the role of
\epsilon
in terms of convergence and performance of adaptive methods.

•

Extensive experiments show that Adam can outperform SGD in tasks where adaptive methods have found little success. As suggested by our theoretical results, tuning
\epsilon
is key to achieving optimal results with adaptive methods.

•

We propose
AvaGrad
, a theoretically-motivated adaptive method that decouples the learning rate
\alpha
and the adaptability parameter
\epsilon
. Quantifying the hyperparameter tuning cost using a zeroth-order method, we observe that AvaGrad is significantly cheaper to tune than Adam.
Matching the generalization accuracy of SGD and other adaptive methods across tasks and domains, AvaGrad offers performance dominance given low tuning budgets.

29.2
Research Directions

Our analysis and empirical results suggest several promising directions for future research. Further investigating optimizer design with a focus on reducing or simplifying hyperparameter tuning costs, for example via adaptive schedules or meta-learned strategies, could further decrease the computational costs of finding optimal configurations for adaptive methods. Another direction is to analyze the generalization properties of adaptive methods and SGD. This could lead to a better understanding of how adaptivity affects generalization gaps, and how to best control it during training in order to balance training speed and final generalization performance.

29.3
Impact

Silva and Rodriguez 2023
propose a method to directly estimate the optimal value for
\epsilon
and hence control an optimizer’s adaptivity by inspecting gradient statistics during training. Their approach circumvents the need to design a hand-crafted schedule for
\epsilon_{t}
, and balances training speed and final generalization performance dynamically and in a task-dependent fashion.

30
Proofs

31
Full Statement and Proof of Theorem
6.1

Theorem
.

For any
\epsilon\geq 0
and constant
\beta_{2,t}=\beta_{2}\in[0,1)
, there is a
stochastic optimization problem for which Adam does not converge to
a stationary point.

Proof.

Consider the following stochastic optimization problem:

\min_{w\in[0,1]}f(w)\coloneqq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s\sim\mathcal{D}}~\left[{f_{s}(w)}\right]\qquad f_{s}(w)=\begin{cases}C\frac{w^{2}}{2},\quad\text{with probability }\quad p\coloneqq\frac{1+\delta}{C+1}\\
-w,\quad\text{otherwise}\end{cases}\,,

(71)

where
\delta
is a positive constant to be specified later, and
C>\frac{1-p}{p}>1+\frac{\epsilon}{w_{1}\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}
is another constant that can depend on
\delta,\beta_{2}
and
\epsilon
, and will also be determined later. Note
that
\nabla f(w)=pCw-(1-p)
, and
f
is minimized at

w^{\star}=\frac{1-p}{Cp}=\frac{C-\delta}{C(1+\delta)}
.

The proof follows closely from
Reddi et al. 2018
. We assume w.l.o.g. that
\beta_{1}=0
. We first consider the difference between two consecutive iterates computed by Adam with a constant learning rate
\alpha
:

\Delta_{t}=w_{t+1}-w_{t}=-\alpha\frac{g_{t}}{\sqrt{v_{t}}+\epsilon}=-\alpha\frac{g_{t}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t}^{2}}+\epsilon}\,,

(72)

and then we proceed to analyze the expected change in iterates divided by the learning rate. First, note that with probability
p
we have
g_{t}=\nabla(C\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{2})=Cw_{t}
, and while
g_{t}=\nabla(-w)=-1
with probability
1-p
. Therefore, we have

\begin{split}\frac{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\Delta_{t}}\right]}{\alpha}&=\frac{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{w_{t+1}-w_{t}}\right]}{\alpha}=-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{g_{t}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t}^{2}}+\epsilon}}\right]\\
&=p~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\underbrace{\frac{-Cw_{t}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2})C^{2}w_{t}^{2}}+\epsilon}}_{T_{1}}}\right]+(1-p)~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\underbrace{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2})}+\epsilon}}_{T_{2}}}\right]\,,\end{split}

(73)

where the expectation is over all the randomness in the algorithm up to time

t
, as all expectations to follow in the proof. We will proceed by computing lower bounds for the terms
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
above. Note that
T_{1}=0
for

w_{t}=0
, while for
w_{t}>0
we can bound
T_{1}
by

\begin{split}T_{1}=\frac{-Cw_{t}}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2})C^{2}w_{t}^{2}}+\epsilon}\geq\frac{-Cw_{t}}{\sqrt{(1-\beta_{2})C^{2}w_{t}^{2}}}=\frac{-1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}\,.\end{split}

(74)

Combining the cases
w_{t}=0
and
w_{t}>0
(note that the feasible region is
w\in[0,1]
), we have that, generally,

T_{1}\geq\min(0,\frac{-1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}})=\frac{-1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}
.

Next, we bound the expected value of
T_{2}
using Jensen’s inequality coupled with the convexity of
x^{-1/2}
as

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{T_{2}}\right]=~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}v_{t-1}+1-\beta_{2}}+\epsilon}}\right]\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{v_{t-1}}\right]+1-\beta_{2}}+\epsilon}\end{split}\,.

(75)

Let us consider
~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{v_{t-1}}\right]
now. Note that

\begin{split}v_{t-1}&=\beta_{2}v_{t-2}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-1}^{2}\\
&=\beta_{2}\left(\beta_{2}v_{t-3}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-2}^{2}\right)+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-1}^{2}\\
&\quad=\beta_{2}^{2}v_{t-3}+\beta_{2}(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-1}^{2}\\
&=\beta_{2}^{2}\left(\beta_{2}v_{t-4}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-3}^{2}\right)+\beta_{2}(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-1}^{2}\\
&\quad=\beta_{2}^{3}v_{t-4}+\beta_{2}^{2}(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-3}^{2}+\beta_{2}(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-1}^{2}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\vdots\\
&=\beta_{2}^{t-1}v_{0}+\beta_{2}^{t-2}(1-\beta_{2})g_{1}^{2}+\beta_{2}^{t-3}(1-\beta_{2})g_{2}^{2}+\dots+(1-\beta_{2})g_{t-1}^{2}\\
&=(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\beta_{2}^{t-i-1}g_{i}^{2}\,,\end{split}

(76)

where we used the fact that
v_{0}=0
(i.e. the second-moment estimate is initialized as zero).

Taking the expectation of the above expression for
v_{t-1}
, we get

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{v_{t-1}}\right]&=(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\beta_{2}^{t-i-1}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{g_{i}^{2}}\right]\\
&=(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\beta_{2}^{t-i-1}\left(1-p+pC^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{w_{t}^{2}}\right]\right)\,,\end{split}

(77)

where we can use the fact that
w_{t}\in[0,1]
, so
w_{t}^{2}\leq 1
to get

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{v_{t-1}}\right]&\leq(1-\beta_{2})\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\beta_{2}^{t-i-1}\left(1-p+pC^{2}\right)\\
&=(1-\beta_{2})\left(1-p+pC^{2}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}\beta_{2}^{t-i-1}\\
&=(1-\beta_{2})\left(1-p+pC^{2}\right)\sum_{i=0}^{t-2}\beta_{2}^{i}\\
&=\left(1-p+pC^{2}\right)\sum_{i=0}^{t-2}\left(\beta_{2}^{i}-\beta_{2}^{i+1}\right)\\
&=\left(1-p+pC^{2}\right)\left(1-\beta_{2}^{t-1}\right)\\
&\leq(1+\delta)C^{2}\,,\end{split}

(78)

where
\sum_{i=0}^{t-2}\left(\beta_{2}^{i}-\beta_{2}^{i+1}\right)=1-\beta_{2}^{t-1}
follows from the fact that the sum telescopes.

Plugging the above bound in (
75
) yields

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{T_{2}}\right]\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}(1+\delta)C+1-\beta_{2}}+\epsilon}\end{split}

(79)

Combining the bounds for
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
in (
73
) gets us that

\begin{split}\frac{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\Delta_{t}}\right]}{\alpha}&\geq\frac{1+\delta}{C+1}\frac{-1}{\sqrt{1-\beta_{2}}}+\left(1-\frac{1+\delta}{C+1}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\beta_{2}(1+\delta)C+1-\beta_{2}}+\epsilon}\end{split}

(80)

Now, recall that
w^{\star}=\frac{C-\delta}{C(1+\delta)}
, so for
C
sufficiently large in comparison to
\delta
we get
w^{\star}\approx\frac{1}{1+\delta}
. On the other hand, the above quantity can be made non-negative for large enough
C
, so
~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{w_{t}}\right]\geq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{w_{t-1}}\right]\geq\dots\geq w_{1}
. In other words, Adam
will, in expectation, update the iterates towards
w=1
even though the stationary point is
w^{*}\approx\frac{1}{1+\delta}
and we have
\left\lVert{\nabla f(1)}\right\rVert^{2}=\delta
at
w=1
. Setting
\delta=1
, for example,
implies that

\lim_{T\to\infty}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]=1
, and hence Adam presents nonconvergence in terms of stationarity. To see that
w=1
is not a stationary point due to the feasibility constraints, check that
\nabla f(1)=1>0
: that is, the negative gradient points
towards
the feasible region.
∎

32
Technical Lemmas

This section presents intermediate results that are used in the proofs given in the next sections.

For simplicity we adopt the following notation for all following results:

H_{t,\infty}=\max_{i}\eta_{t,i}\quad\quad L_{t,\infty}=\min_{i}\eta_{t,i}\,,

(81)

where
\eta_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
denotes the parameter-wise learning rates computed at iteration
t
(the method being considered and consequently the exact expression for
\eta_{t}
will be specified in each result).

For the following Lemmas we rely extensively on the assumption that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
for some constant
G_{2}
, and also that this assumption implies that there exists
G_{2}
such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which can be seen by noting that

\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla f_{s}(w))_{i}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\left(d\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}=\sqrt{d}\cdot\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{d}\cdot G_{2}\,,

(82)

hence such constant
G_{2}
must exist as any
G_{2}\geq\sqrt{d}\cdot G_{2}
satisfies
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume that there exists a constant
G_{2}
such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
G_{2}
be a constant such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Moreover, assume that
\beta_{1,t}\in[0,1)
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let
m_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be given by

m_{t}=\beta_{1,t}m_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{1,t})g_{t}\quad\textrm{and}\quad m_{0}=0\,,

where
\beta_{1,t}\in[0,1)
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

Then, we have

\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all possible sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})\in\mathcal{S}^{t}
.

Proof.

Assume for the sake of contradiction that
\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}>G_{2}
for some
t\in\mathbb{N}
and some sequence of samples
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})
. Moreover, assume w.l.o.g. that
\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
for all
t^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,t-1\}
and note that there is no loss of generality since
(m_{t^{\prime}})_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t}
must indeed have a first element that satisfies
\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}>G_{2}
, which cannot be
m_{0}
since we have
m_{0}=0
by definition.

Then, we have that
m_{t,i}>G_{2}
for some
i\in[d]
, but

\begin{split}m_{t,i}&=\beta_{1,t}m_{t-1,i}+(1-\beta_{1,t})g_{t,i}\\
&\leq\beta_{1,t}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\lVert{g_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&\leq\beta_{1,t}G_{2}+(1-\beta_{1,t})G_{2}\\
&=G_{2}\,,\end{split}

(83)

where we used
\beta_{1,t}\in[0,1)
and the assumptions
\left\lVert{m_{t-1}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
and
\left\lVert{g_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
.

To show that
\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
, note that if we assume
\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert>G_{2}
and
\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
for all
t^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,t-1\}
, we again get a contradiction since, by the triangle inequality,

\begin{split}\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert&=\left\lVert{\beta_{1,t}m_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{1,t})g_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&\leq\beta_{1,t}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}}\right\rVert+(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\lVert{g_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&\leq\beta_{1,t}G_{2}+(1-\beta_{1,t})G_{2}\\
&=G_{2}\,,\end{split}

(84)

therefore it must indeed follow that
\left\lVert{m_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
.

∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume that there exists a constant
G_{2}
such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
G_{2}
be a constant such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Moreover, assume that
\beta_{2,t}\in[0,1)
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

Let
v_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be given by

v_{t}=\beta_{2,t}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2,t})g_{t}^{2}\quad\textrm{and}\quad v_{0}=0\,,

where
\beta_{2,t}\in[0,1)
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

Then, we have

\left\lVert{v_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}^{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\left\lVert{v_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}^{2}

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all possible sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})\in\mathcal{S}^{t}
.

Proof.

The proof follows closely from the one of Lemma
6.1
. Assume for the sake of contradiction that there exists
t\in\mathbb{N}
and some sequence of samples
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})
such that
\left\lVert{v_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}>G_{2}^{2}
and
\left\lVert{v_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}^{2}
for all
t^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,t-1\}
.

Then
v_{t,i}>G_{2}^{2}
for some
i\in[d]
but

\begin{split}v_{t,i}&=\beta_{2,t}v_{t-1,i}+(1-\beta_{2,t})g_{t,i}^{2}\\
&\leq\beta_{2,t}\left\lVert{v_{t-1}}\right\rVert_{\infty}+(1-\beta_{2,t})\left\lVert{g_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\\
&\leq\beta_{2,t}G_{2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{2,t})G_{2}^{2}\\
&=G_{2}^{2}\,,\end{split}

(85)

where we used
\beta_{2,t}\in[0,1)
and the assumptions
\left\lVert{v_{t-1}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}^{2}
and
\left\lVert{g_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
, which raises a contradiction and shows that indeed
\left\lVert{v_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}^{2}
.

For the
\ell_{2}
case, assume that
\left\lVert{v_{t}}\right\rVert>G_{2}^{2}
and
\left\lVert{v_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}^{2}
for all
t^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,t-1\}
, which yields

\begin{split}\left\lVert{v_{t}}\right\rVert&=\left\lVert{\beta_{2,t}v_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{2,t})g_{t}^{2}}\right\rVert\\
&\leq\beta_{2,t}\left\lVert{v_{t-1}}\right\rVert+(1-\beta_{2,t})\left\lVert{g_{t}^{2}}\right\rVert\\
&\leq\beta_{2,t}G_{2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{2,t})G_{2}^{2}\\
&=G_{2}^{2}\,,\end{split}

(86)

where we used the assumption
\left\lVert{g_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
which also implies that

\begin{split}\left\lVert{g_{t}^{2}}\right\rVert&=\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{t,i}^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leq\left[\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{t,i}^{4}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}g_{t,i}^{2}g_{t,j}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&=\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{t,i}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&=\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{t,i}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]^{2}\\
&\leq G_{2}^{2}\,.\end{split}

(87)

Checking that (
86
) yields a contradiction completes the argument.

∎

Lemma 6.3
.

Under the same assumptions of Lemma
6.1
, we have

\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\leq\min\left(G_{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert,G_{2}H_{t,\infty}\right)\,,

(88)

for all
t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and all possible sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{\max(t,t^{\prime})})
.

Proof.

By definition,

\begin{split}\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}&=\sum_{i=1}^{d}m_{t,i}^{2}\cdot\eta_{t,i}^{2}\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\max_{j\in[d]}m_{t^{\prime},j}^{2})\cdot\eta_{t,i}^{2}\\
&\leq\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\eta_{t,i}^{2}\\
&=\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\cdot\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}\,,\end{split}

(89)

hence invoking Lemma
6.1
and taking the square root yields
\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert
.

Additionally, we have

\begin{split}\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}m_{t^{\prime},i}^{2}(\max_{j\in[d]}\eta_{t,j}^{2})\\
&\leq\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{d}m_{t^{\prime},i}^{2}\\
&=\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\cdot\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}}\right\rVert^{2}\,,\end{split}

(90)

hence recalling that
\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert_{\infty}=H_{t,\infty}
and by Lemma
6.1
we get
\left\lVert{m_{t^{\prime}}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}H_{t,\infty}
.

Combining the two bounds completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

Under the same assumptions of Lemma
6.1
, we have

\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle\geq(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle-\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,,

(91)

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all possible sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})\in\mathcal{S}^{t}
.

Proof.

Using the definition of
m_{t}
, we have

\begin{split}\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle&=\Big\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),\big(\beta_{1,t}m_{t-1}+(1-\beta_{1,t})g_{t}\big)\odot\eta_{t}\Big\rangle\\
&=(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle+\beta_{1,t}\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle\\
&\geq(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle-\beta_{1,t}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert\cdot\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,,\end{split}

(92)

where we used Cauchy-Schwarz in the last step.

Next, by Jensen’s inequality and the fact that
\left\lVert{\cdot}\right\rVert
is convex we have, for all
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\left\lVert{\nabla f(w)}\right\rVert=\left\lVert{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s}~\left[{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right]}\right\rVert\leq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s}~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert}\right]\leq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s}~\left[{G_{2}}\right]=G_{2}\,.

(93)

Applying this bound in (
92
) yields the desired inequality.

∎

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume that there exists a constant
G_{2}
such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
G_{2}
be a constant such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Moreover, assume that
\beta_{1,t}\in[0,1)
and
\beta_{1,t}\leq\beta_{1,t-1}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

If
\eta_{t}
is independent of
s_{t}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e.
P(\eta_{t}=\eta,s_{t}=s)=P(\eta_{t}=\eta)P(s_{t}=s)
for all
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},s\in\mathcal{S}
, then

~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle}\right]\geq(1-\beta_{1})L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}-\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,,

(94)

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and all possible sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})\in\mathcal{S}^{t}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
6.4
we have that

\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle\geq(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle-\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,.

(95)

Then, taking the expectation over the draw of
s_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
and recalling that
w_{t}
, and hence also
\nabla f(w_{t})
, is computed from
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t-1})
,

~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle}\right]\geq(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right]\right\rangle-\beta_{1,t}G_{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert}\right]\,.

(96)

Now, note that since we assume that
\eta_{t}
is independent of
s_{t}
, we get

~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right]=\eta_{t}\odot~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{g_{t}}\right]=\eta_{t}\odot~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\nabla f_{s_{t}}(w_{t})}\right]=\eta_{t}\odot\nabla f(w_{t})\,,

(97)

and also

~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert}\right]=\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,.

(98)

Combining (
98
) and (
97
) into (
96
) yields

~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle}\right]\geq(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),\nabla f(w_{t})\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle-\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,.

(99)

Moreover, we have

\begin{split}\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),\nabla f(w_{t})\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle&=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla f(w_{t}))_{i}(\nabla f(w_{t}))_{i}\eta_{t,i}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla f(w_{t}))^{2}_{i}\eta_{t,i}\\
&\geq\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla f(w_{t}))^{2}_{i}(\min_{j}\eta_{t,j})\\
&=L_{t,\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{d}(\nabla f(w_{t}))^{2}_{i}\\
&=L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\,,\end{split}

(100)

which, when applied to (
99
) yields

~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle}\right]\geq(1-\beta_{1,t})L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}-\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\,,

(101)

where we also used that
\beta_{1,t}\in[0,1)
and
L_{t,\infty}\geq 0
. Using the fact that
\beta_{1,t}\leq\beta_{1,t-1}\leq\beta_{1}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and hence
1-\beta_{1,t}\geq 1-\beta_{1}
yields the desired inequality.

∎

33
Proof of Theorem
6.3

We organize the proof as follows: we first prove an intermediate result (Lemma
6.6
) and split the proof of the bounds in (
67
) and (
68
) in two, where the latter can be seen as a refinement of (
67
) given the additional assumption that
Z\coloneqq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\min_{i}\eta_{t,i}
is independent of each
s_{t}
.

Throughout the proof we use the following notation for clarity:

H_{t,\infty}=\max_{i}\eta_{t,i}\quad\quad L_{t,\infty}=\min_{i}\eta_{t,i}\,.

(102)

Lemma 6.6
.

Assume that
f
is
L
-smooth, lower-bounded by some
f^{*}
(i.e.
f^{*}\leq f(w)
for all
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
), and that there exists a constant
G_{2}
such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and let
G_{2}
be a constant such that
\left\lVert{\nabla f_{s}(w)}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}
for all
s\in\mathcal{S}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Consider any optimization method that performs updates following

w_{t+1}=w_{t}-\alpha_{t}\cdot\eta_{t}\odot m_{t}\,,

(103)

where we further assume assume that for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
we have
\alpha_{t}\geq 0
,
\beta_{1,t}=\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{t}}
for some
\beta_{1}\in[0,1)
, and
\eta_{t,i}\geq 0
for all
i\in[d]
.

If
\eta_{t}
is independent of
s_{t}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, i.e.
P(\eta_{t}=\eta,s_{t}=s)=P(\eta_{t}=\eta)P(s_{t}=s)
for all
\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},s\in\mathcal{S}
, then

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&+\frac{L}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(104)

for all
T\in\mathbb{N}
and all possible sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{T})\in\mathcal{S}^{T}
.

Proof.

We start from the assumption that
f
is
L
-smooth, which yields

f(w_{t+1})\leq f(w_{t})+\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),w_{t+1}-w_{t}\rangle+\frac{L}{2}\left\lVert{w_{t+1}-w_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

(105)

Plugging the update expression
w_{t+1}=w_{t}-\alpha_{t}\cdot\eta_{t}\odot m_{t}
,

\begin{split}f(w_{t+1})&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}L}{2}\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.\end{split}

(106)

Now, taking the expectation over the random sample
s_{t}\in\mathcal{S}
, we get

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle}\right]+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}L}{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\,,\end{split}

(107)

where we used the fact that
w_{t}
and
\alpha_{t}
are not functions of of
s_{t}
– in particular, recall that
w_{t}
is deterministically computed from
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t-1})
.

Using the assumption that
\eta_{t}
is independent of
s_{t}
and applying Lemma
6.5
, we get

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}&+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}L}{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\,,\end{split}

(108)

which can be re-arranged into

\begin{split}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]&+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}L}{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(109)

where we used the assumption that
\beta_{1}\in[0,1)
, hence
1-\beta_{1}>0
which was used to divide both sides of the inequality.

Now, summing over
t=1
to
T
,

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}L}{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(110)

which yields the desired result.

∎

33.1
Proof of the first guarantee (Equation
67
)

Proof.

We start from the bound given in Lemma
6.6
:

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&+\frac{L}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(111)

Now, using Lemma
6.3
to upper bound both
\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert
and
\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert
by
G_{2}H_{t,\infty}
,

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}\\
&+\frac{L}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{2}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}^{2}\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(112)

where we used that
~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{H_{t,\infty}^{2}}\right]=H_{t,\infty}^{2}
since
H_{t,\infty}
is deterministically computed from
\eta_{t}
, which in turn is independent of
s_{t}
.

Next, from the assumption in Theorem
6.3
that there are positive constants
L_{\infty}
and
H_{\infty}
such that
L_{\infty}\leq\eta_{t,i}\leq H_{\infty}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N},i\in[d]
and sample sequences
(s_{1},\dots,s_{t})
, it follows that

L_{\infty}\leq L_{t,\infty}=\min_{i\in[d]}\eta_{t,i}\quad\text{ and }\quad H_{\infty}\geq H_{t,\infty}=\max_{i\in[d]}\eta_{t,i}

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, therefore

\begin{split}L_{\infty}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+G_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}\\
&+\frac{LG_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}^{2}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{2}\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(113)

Dividing both sides by
L_{\infty}\geq 0
and letting
\alpha_{t}=\alpha^{\prime}/\sqrt{T}
yields

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{L_{\infty}(1-\beta_{1})}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+G_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\beta_{1,t}\\
&+\frac{LG_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}^{2}}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha^{\prime 2}}{T}\Bigg)\,,\end{split}

(114)

and, rearranging and using the fact that

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\beta_{1,t}=\beta_{1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\leq\beta_{1}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}dt\leq 2\beta_{1}\sqrt{T}\,,

which implies that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\beta_{1,t}\leq 2\alpha^{\prime}\beta_{1}
, we get

\begin{split}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{L_{\infty}(1-\beta_{1})}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+2\alpha^{\prime}\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}\\
&+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 2}LG_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}^{2}}{2}\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(115)

Now, taking the expectation over the full sample sequence
(s_{1},\dots,s_{T})
yields

\begin{split}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{L_{\infty}(1-\beta_{1})}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t})}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+2\alpha^{\prime}\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}\\
&+\frac{\alpha^{\prime 2}LG_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}^{2}}{2}\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(116)

Note that, by telescoping sum,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t})}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]=~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{1})}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{T+1})}\right]\leq f(w_{1})-f^{*}\,,

(117)

where the last step follows since
w_{1}
(the parameters at initialization) is independent of the drawn samples, and also from the assumption that
f^{*}
lower bounds
f
.

Combining the above with (
116
) and dividing both sides by
\alpha^{\prime}\cdot\sqrt{T}
,

\begin{split}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq\frac{1}{L_{\infty}\sqrt{T}(1-\beta_{1})}\Bigg(\frac{f(w_{1})-f^{*}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+2\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}LG_{2}^{2}H_{\infty}^{2}}{2}\Bigg),.\end{split}

(118)

Finally, we will use Young’s inequality with
p=2
and the conjugate exponent
q=2
, which states that
xy\leq\frac{x^{2}}{2}+\frac{y^{2}}{2}
for any nonnegative numbers
x,y
.

In that context, let

x=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}}}\quad\text{ and }\quad y=\sqrt{\alpha^{\prime}}H_{\infty}\,,

(119)

which yields

H_{\infty}=xy\leq\frac{x^{2}}{2}+\frac{y^{2}}{2}=\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\alpha^{\prime}H_{\infty}^{2}\,,

(120)

and hence

2\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}\cdot H_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+2\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}\cdot\alpha^{\prime}H_{\infty}^{2}\,.

(121)

Plugging the above in (
118
) yields

\begin{split}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq\frac{1}{L_{\infty}\sqrt{T}(1-\beta_{1})}\Bigg(\frac{2\beta_{1}G_{2}^{2}+f(w_{1})-f^{*}}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\alpha^{\prime}H_{\infty}^{2}\frac{G_{2}^{2}(L+2\beta_{1})}{2}\Bigg),.\end{split}

(122)

Verifying that the above is
\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{L\sqrt{T}}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha^{\prime}}+\alpha^{\prime}H_{\infty}^{2}\right)\right)
in terms of
T,\alpha^{\prime},L_{\infty}
and
H_{\infty}
finishes the proof.

∎

33.2
Proof of the second guarantee (Equation
68
)

Proof.

As before, we start from Lemma
6.6
, which states that

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\\
&+\frac{L}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(123)

We then invoke Lemma
6.3
to upper bound
\left\lVert{m_{t-1}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert
by
G_{2}H_{t,\infty}
and
\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert
by
G_{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}
:

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta_{1}}\Bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]\right)&+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}\\
&+\frac{L}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{2}G_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\Bigg)\,.\end{split}

(124)

Next, define the unormalized probability distribution
\tilde{p}(t)=\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}
, so that
p(t)=\tilde{p}(t)/Z
with
Z=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{p}(t)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}
is a valid distribution over
t\in\{1,\dots T\}
. Dividing both sides by
Z
yields

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}&\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]}{2}\right)\end{split}

(125)

Now, taking the conditional expectation over all samples
S
given
Z
:

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\Big|Z}\right]&\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\big(~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t})|Z}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]|Z}\right]\big)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]}{2}\Big|Z}\right]\Big)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\Big(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\big(~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t})|Z}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t+1})|Z}\right]\big)\\
&\qquad+\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\Big|Z}\right]\Big)\\
&=\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\Big(f(w_{1})-f^{*}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\Big|Z}\right]\Big)\,.\end{split}

(126)

where in the second step we used
~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\cdot}\right]|Z}\right]=~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\cdot|Z}\right]
which follows from the assumption that
p(Z|s_{t})=p(Z)
, and the third step follows from telescoping sum and the assumption that
f^{*}
lower bounds
f
.

Then, taking the expectation over
Z
and re-arranging:

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{f(w_{1})-f^{*}}{T}+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\right)}\right]\,.\end{split}

(127)

Setting
\beta_{1}=0
for simplicity yields

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{1}{Z}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{f(w_{1})-f^{*}}{T}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\right)}\right]\,.\end{split}

(128)

Now, let
\alpha_{t}=\alpha_{t}^{\prime}/\sqrt{T}

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{1}{Z}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{f(w_{1})-f^{*}}{T}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{\prime 2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2T}\right)}\right]\\
&=\frac{1}{T}\cdot~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{1}{Z}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f(w_{1})-f^{*}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{t}^{\prime 2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}\right)}\right]\,.\end{split}

(129)

Now, recall that
Z=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}L_{t,\infty}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{\prime}L_{t,\infty}
, hence

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\cdot~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}f(w_{1})-f^{*}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{t}^{\prime 2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{\prime}L_{t,\infty}}}\right]\\
&\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\cdot~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}1+\alpha_{t}^{\prime 2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{\prime}L_{t,\infty}}}\right]\right)\,.\end{split}

(130)

Finally, checking that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}=~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{t\sim\mathcal{P}(t|S)}~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]
:

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\sim\mathcal{D}^{T}\\
t\sim\mathcal{P}(t|s)\end{subarray}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\cdot~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T}1+\alpha_{t}^{\prime 2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\alpha_{t}^{\prime}L_{t,\infty}}}\right]\right)\,.\end{split}

(131)

Recalling that
L_{t,\infty}=\min_{i}\eta_{t,i}
completes the proof.
∎

34
Full Statement and Proof of Theorem
6.2

We organize the formal statement and proof of Theorem
6.2
as follows: we first state a general convergence result for Adam which depends on the step-wise adaptivity parameter
\epsilon_{t}
and the learning rates
\alpha_{t}
in Theorem
6.2
, and then present a Corollary that shows how a
\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{T})
rate follows from such result (Corollary
Corollary
). This section proceeds the proof of Theorem
6.3
(Appendix
33
) as the proof presented here is more easily seen as a small variant (although overall simpler) of the analysis given in the previous section. Steps which also appear in the proof of Theorem
6.3
are not necessarily described in full detail, hence the following arguments can be better understood with the previous section in context.

Throughout the proof we use the following notation for clarity:

H_{t,\infty}=\max_{i}\eta_{t,i}\quad\quad L_{t,\infty}=\min_{i}\eta_{t,i}\,.

(132)

Theorem
.

Assume that
f
is smooth and
f_{s}
has bounded gradients. If
\epsilon_{t}\geq\epsilon_{t-1}>0
for all
t\in[T]
, then for the iterates
\{w_{1},\dots,w_{T}\}
produced by Adam we have

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\left(1+\alpha_{t}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}\right)}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1+\epsilon_{t-1}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(133)

where
w_{t}
is sampled from
p(t)\propto\frac{\alpha_{t}}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}
.

Corollary
.

Setting
\epsilon_{t}=\Theta(T^{p_{1}}t^{p_{2}})
for any
p_{1},p_{2}>0
such that
p_{1}+p_{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
(e.g.
\epsilon_{t}=\Theta(\sqrt{T}),\epsilon_{t}=\Theta(\sqrt[4]{Tt}),\epsilon_{t}=\Theta(\sqrt{t})
) and
\alpha_{t}=\Theta\left(\frac{\epsilon_{t}}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
on Theorem
6.2
yields a bound of
\mathcal{O}(1/\sqrt{T})
for Adam.

Proof.

Similarly to the proof of Theorem
6.3
, we plug the update rule
w_{t+1}=w_{t}-\alpha_{t}\cdot\eta_{t}\odot m_{t}
in

f(w_{t+1})\leq f(w_{t})+\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),w_{t+1}-w_{t}\rangle+\frac{L}{2}\left\lVert{w_{t+1}-w_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

(134)

yielding

\begin{split}f(w_{t+1})&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}L}{2}\left\lVert{m_{t}\odot\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}\,.\end{split}

(135)

By Lemmas
6.3
and
6.4
, we have

\begin{split}f(w_{t+1})&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\right\rangle+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\,.\end{split}

(136)

Now, note that we can write

\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t}\rangle=\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t-1}\rangle+\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})\rangle\,,

therefore we have that

\begin{split}f(w_{t+1})&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t-1}\right\rangle+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\\
&\quad\quad-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})\right\rangle\\
&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t-1}\right\rangle+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\\
&\quad\quad+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left|\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})\right\rangle\right|\end{split}

(137)

We will proceed to bound
\left|\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})\right\rangle\right|
. By Cauchy-Schwarz we have

\left|\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})\right\rangle\right|\leq\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert\cdot\left\lVert{g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})}\right\rVert\leq G_{2}\left\lVert{g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})}\right\rVert\,,

(138)

and moreover

\begin{split}\left\lVert{g_{t}\odot(\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1})}\right\rVert&=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}g_{t,i}^{2}|\eta_{t,i}-\eta_{t-1,i}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}G_{2}^{2}|\eta_{t,i}-\eta_{t-1,i}|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\\
&=G_{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert\,,\end{split}

(139)

therefore we get

\begin{split}f(w_{t+1})&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),g_{t}\odot\eta_{t-1}\right\rangle+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}H_{t,\infty}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2}\\
&\quad\quad+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})G_{2}G_{2}\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert\end{split}

(140)

Using the fact that
\eta_{t-1}
is independent of
s_{t}
and that
~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{g_{t}}\right]=\nabla f(w_{t})
, taking expectation over
s_{t}
yields

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),\nabla f(w_{t})\odot\eta_{t-1}\right\rangle+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{H_{t,\infty}}\right]\\
&\quad\quad+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]}{2}+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1,t})G_{2}G_{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert}\right]\\
&\leq f(w_{t})-\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})\left\lVert{\nabla f(w)}\right\rVert^{2}L_{t-1}+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{H_{t,\infty}}\right]\\
&\quad\quad+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]}{2}+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})G_{2}G_{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert}\right]\,,\end{split}

(141)

where in the second step we used

\beta_{1,t}\leq\beta_{1}
and

\left\langle\nabla f(w_{t}),\nabla f(w_{t})\odot\eta_{t-1}\right\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\nabla f(w)_{i}^{2}\eta_{t-1,i}\geq\min_{j}\eta_{t-1,j}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\nabla f(w)_{i}^{2}=L_{t-1}\left\lVert{\nabla f(w)}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

Re-arranging,

\begin{split}\alpha_{t}L_{t-1}(1-\beta_{1})\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}&\leq f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]+\alpha_{t}\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{H_{t,\infty}}\right]\\
&\quad\quad+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LG_{2}^{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]}{2}+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})G_{2}G_{2}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert}\right]\,,\end{split}

(142)

Next, we will bound
L_{t-1},H_{t},\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert
, and
\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert
. Recall that, for Adam, we have

\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{v_{t}}+\epsilon_{t}}\,,

and since
v_{t,i}\leq G_{2}^{2}
, we also have that

\frac{1}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}}\leq\eta_{t,i}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{t}}\,.

From the above it follows that

\frac{1}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}\leq L_{t-1}

and

H_{t,\infty}\geq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{t}}\,,

which also implies that
\left\lVert{\eta_{t}}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{t}}
.

As for
\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert
, check that

\Big|\eta_{t,i}-\eta_{t-1,i}\Big|\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{t-1}}-\frac{1}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}}=\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{G_{2}\epsilon_{t-1}+\epsilon_{t}\epsilon_{t-1}}\leq\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\,,

(143)

where we used the assumption that
\epsilon_{t}\geq\epsilon_{t-1}
. The above implies that
\left\lVert{\eta_{t}-\eta_{t-1}}\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{d}\cdot\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}
.

Applying the bounds given above to (
142
) yields

\begin{split}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}(1-\beta_{1})\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}&\leq f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]+\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LdG_{2}^{2}}{\epsilon_{t}^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{d}G_{2}G_{2}\cdot\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(144)

Next, define the unormalized probability distribution
\tilde{p}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}
, so that
p(t)=\tilde{p}(t)/Z
with
Z=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{p}(t)=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}
is a valid distribution over
t\in\{1,\dots T\}
. Adopting this notation and dividing both sides by
Z(1-\beta_{1})
:

\begin{split}p(t)\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}&\Big(f(w_{t})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{s_{t}}~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]+\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LdG_{2}^{2}}{\epsilon_{t}^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{d}G_{2}G_{2}\cdot\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\Big)\,.\end{split}

(145)

Taking the expectation over all samples and summing over
t
yields

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t})}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]+\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LdG_{2}^{2}}{\epsilon_{t}^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{d}G_{2}G_{2}\cdot\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\Big)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\Big[f(w_{1})-f^{*}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\beta_{1,t}G_{2}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t}}+\frac{\alpha_{t}^{2}LdG_{2}^{2}}{\epsilon_{t}^{2}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\alpha_{t}(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{d}G_{2}G_{2}\cdot\frac{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\Big)\Big]\,.\end{split}

(146)

where we used the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t})}\right]-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{t+1})}\right]=f(w_{1})-~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{f(w_{T+1})}\right]\leq f(w_{1})-f^{*}
by telescoping sum and where
f^{*}
lower bounds
f
.

For simplicity, assume that
\beta_{1,t}=0
(or, alternatively, let
\beta_{1,t}=\frac{\beta_{1}}{\sqrt{T}}
and apply Young’s inequality as in the proof of Theorem
6.3
). In this case, we get

\begin{split}\sum_{t=1}^{T}p(t)~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]&\leq\frac{1}{Z(1-\beta_{1})}\Big[f(w_{1})-f^{*}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\Big(\alpha_{t}LdG_{2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{d}G_{2}G_{2}\cdot\left(G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}\right)\Big)\Big]\,,\end{split}

(147)

and recalling that
Z=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}
yields

\begin{split}~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits_{t\sim P(t)}~\left[{~\mathop{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\large$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\mathbb{E}$}}}{\mathbb{E}}{\mathbb{E}}}\displaylimits~\left[{\left\lVert{\nabla f(w_{t})}\right\rVert^{2}}\right]}\right]&\leq\frac{f(w_{1})-f^{*}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\Big(\alpha_{t}LdG_{2}^{2}+(1-\beta_{1})\sqrt{d}G_{2}G_{2}\cdot\left(G_{2}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}\right)\Big)}{(1-\beta_{1})\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{G_{2}+\epsilon_{t-1}}}\\
&\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{1+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{\epsilon_{t-1}^{2}}\left(1+\alpha_{t}+\epsilon_{t}-\epsilon_{t-1}\right)}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1+\epsilon_{t-1}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(148)

which completes the argument.
∎
